Jun 5

Synthesized hierarchical role restructuring and operational workflow refinements
Synthesized hierarchical role restructuring and operational workflow refinements
Here's my understanding of everything you've shared. Let me break it down clearly.

Role Hierarchy (Revised)

Super Admin -- No trip creation. Responsibilities are system-wide administration, onboarding users, creating states, creating stakeholders, and setting pricing/business rules.
State Admin -- No trip creation either. Responsible for onboarding Trip Admins within their state, and approving or rejecting trip cancellation requests.
Trip Admin (new role) -- The ones who actually create trips, onboard drivers, add vehicle numbers, manage trips within their locality, and initiate cancellation requests.
Driver Onboarding (Revised)

No login accounts for drivers.
Only three fields captured: full name, license number, phone number.
Once onboarded, they persist in the system.
When creating a trip, there's a search to check if the driver already exists -- assign via dropdown if yes, or trigger a registration flow if not.
Pricing / Business Rules

Loading and unloading costs are no longer entered manually on the form.
Super Admin defines a pricing chart, and Trip Admins select from it via a dropdown.
Trip Cancellation Flow

Trip Admin submits a cancellation request with a reason.
It goes to the State Admin for approval or rejection.
What Stays the Same

Wallet, leisure, stakeholder system, and stakeholder payments remain unchanged.
Open Question You Raised

For stakeholder payment amounts, my suggestion would be to let the Super Admin define it through the pricing/business rules chart, similar to loading and unloading. This keeps pricing consistent, prevents arbitrary entries, and reduces errors at the Trip Admin level. But this is worth flagging in the SOW as a point to confirm with the client.

Synthesized three key feature modifications for client documentation
Got it. Here is what I've understood from the new changes:

Public Lookup Page

No functional change from the original plan. Clarification added that each trip has a unique Trip ID, and the public lookup page uses that ID to track and display trip information throughout the trip's lifecycle.

Trip Cancellation - Pre-approved Override

The existing cancellation flow (Trip Admin requests, State Admin approves) remains the default. However, when a State Admin is onboarding a Trip Admin, there should be a toggle or checkbox that grants that specific Trip Admin the ability to cancel trips without needing State Admin approval. So cancellation authority is decided at the point of onboarding, not during the cancellation itself.

Super Admin - Stakeholder Management (Expanded)

Super Admin now has two responsibilities here that should be made explicit:

Onboard third-party stakeholders onto the platform.
Set the payment rates for those stakeholders, alongside the loading and unloading rate sheet.
So the Super Admin is the single source of truth for all rates and all stakeholder configuration. Trip Admins simply select from what Super Admin has defined.

Weighed whether stakeholders need login access before adding them hierarchically
Weighed whether stakeholders need login access before adding them hierarchically
That's a good point to think through carefully. Before I add them, the key question is: do stakeholders have login accounts on the platform?

If they do, then yes, they belong in the role hierarchy table. They would be able to log in and likely view their payment history or ledger entries, which makes them an active role in the system.

If they don't have accounts and are purely passive recipients managed by Super Admin, then they shouldn't be in the role table. They would be more like a configuration entity similar to how drivers are handled, just stored and referenced rather than interacting with the platform.

The reason this matters for the document is that the role table sets expectations with the client about who gets login credentials and what they can do. Adding stakeholders there implies they will have accounts.

What's your understanding -- do stakeholders log in, or are they just recipients that Super Admin configures in the background?

If the driver doesn't have an account, then who will be changing the trip statuses?

Jun 11

Identified architectural gap regarding driver status update permissions
Identified architectural gap regarding driver status update permissions
That is a significant gap we have missed. Good catch.

In Phase 2, the driver trip view with Start and Complete actions was built assuming drivers had login accounts. With that removed, there is now no one assigned to trigger the In Transit and Completed status changes.

There are three realistic options here:

Trip Admin changes the status -- The Trip Admin manually updates the trip status on the driver's behalf, based on a phone call or physical confirmation. Simple, no new UI needed, but relies on the Trip Admin being reachable and responsive.
A public no-login driver interface -- A lightweight screen accessible via a link or the Trip ID (possibly shared with the driver via SMS) where the driver can tap Start and Complete without needing a full account. No credentials, just Trip ID verification.
Driver gets a limited account -- A minimal login (phone number + OTP perhaps) that only gives access to their active trip and the Start/Complete actions. Not a full account but enough to act on trips assigned to them.
Option 1 is the simplest but puts operational burden on Trip Admins. Options 2 and 3 keep the driver involved without the overhead of full account management.

Which direction feels right to you? This will need to go into the SOW before you send it to the client since it directly affects the driver experience and the trip lifecycle.

option 1 seems reasonable for now

Jun 14
Honest opinion -- yes, refactor first is the right call, and here is why.

The old code has assumptions baked in that directly conflict with the new requirements. Super Admin creating trips, drivers having accounts, manual price entry on the trip form -- if you start building new features on top of that, you are building on a broken foundation. You will hit conflicts mid-development and end up doing the cleanup anyway, but in a messier state.

However I want to flag one risk with the refactor-first approach: scope creep during refactoring. It is easy for a refactor to balloon when you are deep in the code. To keep it controlled, I would recommend we define the refactor work very precisely -- a strict list of what gets removed, what gets renamed, and what gets restructured -- before handing anything to the Replit Agent. The agent should know exactly what it is and is not touching.

My suggested approach:

Stage 1 -- Refactor and cleanup

Remove Super Admin trip creation from UI and backend
Remove driver login accounts and auth flows
Refactor single Admin role into State Admin and Trip Admin with correct navigation shells and permissions
Remove manual price entry from trip creation form
Stage 2 -- Build new features

Super Admin pricing chart management
Vehicle management entity and search flow
Driver search and inline registration within trip creation
Cancellation request and approval flow
Stakeholder login and ledger view
Public lookup with payment verification
Pre-approved cancellation toggle on Trip Admin onboarding
Stage 3 -- Phase 3 original plan

UI polish, edge case QA, summary dashboard, demo seeding, Expo Go delivery
Does this structure feel right? If yes I will break Stage 1 and Stage 2 down into precise executable tasks we can hand to the Replit Agent one at a time.

Okay. This looks fine. I think we should first start with stage one, then two, and then three. Let's proceed with, stage one first.

Here are the findings — no changes made.
1. Roles defined in the schema
The lib/db/src/schema/index.ts is empty (just a scaffold template). The actual schema lives in raw SQL inside:
/home/runner/workspace/artifacts/api-server/src/db/migrate.ts
Roles are not defined as a PostgreSQL enum. The users table stores role as VARCHAR(50). The role values used across the codebase are:
super_admin
admin
driver
There is no CHECK constraint or enum enforcing these — any string is technically valid at the DB level.
2. users table structure (from migrate.ts)
Column    Type    Notes
id    UUID    PK, gen_random_uuid()
email    VARCHAR(255)    UNIQUE, NOT NULL
password_hash    TEXT    NOT NULL
role    VARCHAR(50)    NOT NULL — stores super_admin, admin, driver
entity_type    VARCHAR(50)    nullable — state or stakeholder
entity_id    UUID    nullable — FK to states or stakeholders (polymorphic)
is_active    BOOLEAN    default true
deleted_at    TIMESTAMP    soft-delete
created_at    TIMESTAMP    default NOW()
updated_at    TIMESTAMP    default NOW()
Note: There is no dedicated drivers table. Drivers are rows in the users table with role = 'driver'.
3. Auth middleware structure
auth.ts — only does one thing: verifies the Bearer JWT and attaches req.user (a TokenPayload) to the request. It does not check roles.
roleGuard.ts — a separate requireRole(...roles: string[]) factory. It reads req.user.role and returns 403 if the role isn't in the allowed list. Used as a second middleware chained after authenticateToken.
Permission model: flat, string-based role check. No hierarchy, no scope system.
4. Navigation roles in _layout.tsx
Three roles are handled, each mapped to a route group:
Role    Route Group    Screens (from filesystem)
super_admin    /(superadmin)    index, create-trip, trip-detail, users, config
admin    /(admin)    index, ledger
driver    /(driver)    index
Unauthenticated users → /(auth)/login. The /(public) group (lookup screen) is accessible without login. The /(tabs) group exists in the Stack declaration but no role is routed there.
5. Trip creation endpoint — permitted roles
POST /trips    →  authenticateToken + requireRole("super_admin")
Only super_admin can create trips. admin and driver are explicitly excluded from this endpoint.
6. How loading/unloading amounts are captured
Both are manual free-text fields on the frontend. In create-trip.tsx:
loadingFee — plain TextInput with keyboardType="decimal-pad", labelled "Loading Fee (₦)", appended with the selected loading state name
unloadingFee — same pattern, labelled "Unloading Fee (₦)", appended with the selected unloading state name
On submit, both are converted from Naira (decimal string) to kobo (integer) via Math.round(parseFloat(val) * 100) before being sent to the API. There is no lookup, default, or auto-population — the super admin types the amount every time.

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS drivers (
  id             UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  full_name      VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
  license_number VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  phone_number   VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
  created_by     UUID REFERENCES users(id),
  is_active      BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
  deleted_at     TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,
  created_at     TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
  updated_at     TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
)

Identified two critical backend logic flaws requiring immediate remediation
Good results overall. Four checks passed cleanly. Two issues need fixing before we move to Stage 2 -- both are straightforward.

Issue 1 -- drivers table not persisting via migrate.ts

The table was created manually this time, but if the database resets, it will be gone again because the migration script never ran properly due to a TTY prompt blocking it. We need to fix this so the migration is reliable going forward.

Issue 2 -- two dead driver references in tripController.ts

These are broken logic that will silently fail in production. Line 93 validates a user has role = 'driver' when assigning a driver to a trip -- that will never match since no user has that role anymore. Line 720 checks if the caller has role = 'driver' to guard trip access -- drivers no longer have accounts so this guard is meaningless.

Both need to be cleaned up now before Stage 2 builds on top of them. Paste this into the Replit Agent:

Two cleanup items from the Stage 1 test report. Do not make any other changes.

Fix 1 -- Ensure migration script runs reliably

Open artifacts/api-server/src/db/migrate.ts. The drivers table CREATE TABLE block was added in a previous task but the migration was not actually running due to a TTY prompt from drizzle-kit. Confirm that the CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS drivers block exists directly in the migration script as raw SQL (not via drizzle-kit push). If it does, run the migration script directly with:

cd /home/runner/workspace && pnpm --filter @workspace/api-server run migrate
Confirm the script runs to completion and prints ✅ All 9 tables created successfully. with no TTY prompts or interactive steps required.

Fix 2 -- Remove dead driver role logic in tripController.ts

Open artifacts/api-server/src/controllers/tripController.ts and make these two changes:

Line 93 -- There is a SQL query that validates a user exists with role = 'driver'. This check is now invalid since drivers are no longer platform users. Remove or replace this validation. The correct approach going forward is to validate that the assigned driver_id exists in the drivers table rather than the users table. Replace the query so it checks:

sql
SELECT id FROM drivers WHERE id = $1 AND is_active = true AND deleted_at IS NULL
Line 720 -- There is an auth guard that checks if (role === "driver"). Since drivers no longer have platform accounts and cannot call any API endpoint, this guard will never be reached. Remove this entire conditional block.

Validated feature ordering through dependency analysis
Validated feature ordering through dependency analysis
Yes, the order is deliberate and the dependencies are correctly sequenced. Here is why each sits where it does:

Pricing chart first because trip creation in Feature 4 pulls directly from it. You cannot build the trip form without the chart existing.
User onboarding second because you need real state_admin, trip_admin, and stakeholder accounts in the system to test every feature that follows.
Vehicle management third because the trip creation form in Feature 4 needs the vehicle search component ready to plug in.
Trip creation fourth because it depends on all three above -- pricing chart, a trip_admin account to test with, and vehicle search.
Cancellation flow fifth because you need real trips created in Feature 4 before you can test cancellation requests.
State Admin screens sixth because the cancellation approval queue depends on cancellation requests existing from Feature 5. The pre-approved toggle also lives here.
Stakeholder screens seventh because stakeholders are passive -- they just view what has already happened, so their screens can be built once the core transaction data is in place.
Public lookup last because it is read-only and most useful once there is real trip and payment data to display.
The one thing to keep in mind as we go: Features 5 and 6 are tightly connected. The backend for the cancellation flow and the State Admin approval queue will likely be done together even if the frontend work is split across two tasks.

Architected comprehensive pricing chart system with database schema and API routes
Architected comprehensive pricing chart system with database schema and API routes
Good. Everything is greenfield which means we design it cleanly without worrying about what to preserve. Before I write the agent prompt, let me share the design so you can approve it first.

Pricing chart design:

Two new database tables:

pricing_charts -- one active chart at a time, storing:

Loading fee (kobo)
Unloading fee (kobo)
is_active flag
created_by (Super Admin who set it)
pricing_chart_stakeholder_rates -- one row per stakeholder per chart:

pricing_chart_id
stakeholder_id
amount (kobo)
Why two tables? Loading and unloading are fixed platform-wide rates. Stakeholder rates vary per stakeholder, so they need their own rows.

Backend endpoints (all Super Admin only except one):

POST /pricing -- create a new chart with stakeholder rates
PUT /pricing/:id -- update an existing chart
GET /pricing/active -- fetch the current active chart (accessible to trip_admin too, since they need it for trip creation)
POST /pricing/:id/activate -- set a chart as the active one
Frontend:

Replace the read-only config.tsx with a proper pricing management screen where Super Admin can set loading fee, unloading fee, and rates per stakeholder.
Key rule: When a trip is created, rates are copied from the active chart into trip_fees at that moment. If the chart changes later, existing trips are unaffected.

Orchestrated comprehensive prompt structure for admin pricing feature
Orchestrated comprehensive prompt structure for admin pricing feature
Paste this into the Replit Agent:

I need you to build the Super Admin pricing chart feature. This is a greenfield addition -- no existing pricing tables, routes, or screens exist. Follow these steps exactly and in order.

Step 1 -- Database migration

Open artifacts/api-server/src/db/migrate.ts. Add two new tables after the stakeholders table block:

sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pricing_charts (
  id              UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  loading_fee     INTEGER NOT NULL,
  unloading_fee   INTEGER NOT NULL,
  is_active       BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false,
  created_by      UUID REFERENCES users(id),
  created_at      TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
  updated_at      TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);

CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS pricing_chart_stakeholder_rates (
  id               UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  pricing_chart_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES pricing_charts(id),
  stakeholder_id   UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES stakeholders(id),
  amount           INTEGER NOT NULL,
  created_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
  updated_at       TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
  UNIQUE(pricing_chart_id, stakeholder_id)
);
Update the final success log from All 9 tables to All 11 tables. Then run the migration:

pnpm --filter @workspace/api-server run migrate
Confirm both tables are created successfully.

Step 2 -- Backend controller

Create a new file at artifacts/api-server/src/controllers/pricingController.ts with four functions:

createPricingChart -- POST handler. Accepts loading_fee, unloading_fee, and stakeholder_rates (array of { stakeholder_id, amount }). All amounts arrive in kobo as integers. Inserts into pricing_charts with is_active = false, then inserts one row per stakeholder into pricing_chart_stakeholder_rates. Returns the created chart with its stakeholder rates.

updatePricingChart -- PUT handler. Accepts same body as create. Updates the chart row and upserts the stakeholder rates. Only allowed if the chart is not currently active.

getActivePricingChart -- GET handler. Returns the single chart where is_active = true, joined with all its stakeholder rates. Also join the stakeholder name from the stakeholders table. Returns 404 if no active chart exists.

activatePricingChart -- POST handler for /pricing/:id/activate. Sets the specified chart to is_active = true and sets all other charts to is_active = false. Returns the activated chart.

All amounts must remain as integers in kobo throughout -- no conversion to Naira in the backend.

Step 3 -- Backend route

Create artifacts/api-server/src/routes/pricing.ts with these endpoints:

POST   /pricing            authenticateToken + requireRole("super_admin")  → createPricingChart
PUT    /pricing/:id        authenticateToken + requireRole("super_admin")  → updatePricingChart
POST   /pricing/:id/activate  authenticateToken + requireRole("super_admin")  → activatePricingChart
GET    /pricing/active     authenticateToken + requireRole("super_admin", "trip_admin")  → getActivePricingChart
Register this router in artifacts/api-server/src/routes/index.ts under the /pricing prefix.

Step 4 -- Frontend pricing management screen

Replace the content of artifacts/mobile/app/(superadmin)/config.tsx with a proper pricing management screen. The screen should:

On load, fetch GET /pricing/active and display the current active chart if one exists, showing loading fee, unloading fee, and each stakeholder rate in a readable card format. Amounts should be displayed in Naira using the existing utils/formatCurrency.ts utility.
Show a form below (or on a separate tab within the screen) where Super Admin can:
Enter loading fee in Naira (convert to kobo on submit via Math.round(val * 100))
Enter unloading fee in Naira (same conversion)
For each stakeholder fetched from GET /api/stakeholders, enter a rate in Naira
Submit to POST /pricing to create a new chart
Show an Activate button next to any inactive chart to call POST /pricing/:id/activate
Follow the existing design system: white background, primary color 
#1a6ec8, card-based layout, bold typography. Match the visual style of the existing users.tsx screen for consistency.
Do not modify the tab bar or navigation -- config.tsx is already registered as the Config tab in (superadmin)/_layout.tsx.

Step 5 -- TypeScript check

Run pnpm --filter @workspace/api-server exec tsc --noEmit and pnpm --filter @workspace/mobile exec tsc --noEmit. Both must return zero errors.

Once done, confirm:

The database currently has users with stale role values (admin, driver) that no longer exist in the system. Run the following SQL updates directly against the database:

sql
-- Update admin users: those linked to a state become state_admin
UPDATE users SET role = 'state_admin' WHERE role = 'admin' AND entity_type = 'state';

-- Update admin users: those linked to a stakeholder become stakeholder
UPDATE users SET role = 'stakeholder' WHERE role = 'admin' AND entity_type = 'stakeholder';

-- Remove driver users entirely (soft delete)

Out of scope

Editing or deactivating existing users
Self-service registration (all creation is admin-initiated)
Password reset / change password flow
trip_admin creation from the Super Admin screen (only State Admin can create those)
Steps

Fix seed data — Run three SQL UPDATE statements to migrate stale admin rows to state_admin or stakeholder based on entity_type, and soft-delete driver rows. Confirm with a SELECT after.
Backend: createUser controller — Add createUser to the existing userController.ts. Accept email, password, role, entity_type (optional), entity_id (optional). Validate role is one of the four known values. Enforce entity-linking rules per role (state_admin/trip_admin → entity_type = 'state'; stakeholder → entity_type = 'stakeholder'; super_admin → no entity). Validate that entity_id actually exists in the correct table. Enforce caller-role restrictions (super_admin may not create trip_admin; state_admin may only create trip_admin linked to their own entity_id). Hash password with bcrypt before insert. Return the created user without the password hash.
Backend: add POST route — Add POST / to routes/users.ts behind authenticateToken + requireRole("super_admin", "state_admin") pointing at createUser.
Mobile: API helper — Add createUser(body) to services/api.ts calling POST /api/users.
Mobile: create-user form in users.tsx — Add a Create User button (top-right header area, primary blue, consistent with design system). Tapping opens a modal with email, masked password, role picker (State Admin / Stakeholder only for super_admin callers), and a conditional entity dropdown (states list or stakeholders list depending on role). On submit call createUser; on success close modal and refresh the list; on error show the API message inline.
TypeScript check — Run both tsc --noEmit checks; both must return zero errors. Confirm that trips.ts, tripController.ts, pricing.ts, and pricingController.ts were not touched.

Fixes Applied
Fix 1 — Dead create-trip route removed
File: artifacts/mobile/app/(superadmin)/_layout.tsx
Removed the <Tabs.Screen name="create-trip" …/> declaration. The file create-trip.tsx doesn't exist on disk, so this declaration was causing the Tabs navigator's route list to be mismatched — firing a [Layout children] warning on every render and corrupting navigation.navigate() so tab presses silently failed.
Fix 2 — PillTabBar slider pointer events fixed
File: artifacts/mobile/components/PillTabBar.tsx
Added pointerEvents="none" to the Animated.View slider. Without this, the absolutely-positioned sliding pill was a real DOM element that swallowed touch events over the active tab slot in React Native Web.
Fix 3 — _layout.tsx added to three role groups
Files created:
artifacts/mobile/app/(stateadmin)/_layout.tsx
artifacts/mobile/app/(tripadmin)/_layout.tsx
artifacts/mobile/app/(stakeholder)/_layout.tsx

Here are the findings across all five points:
1 — Database: Vehicles Table
No vehicles table exists. There is no CREATE TABLE vehicles anywhere in migrate.ts. The only vehicle reference in the entire migration is a single text column inside the trips table (see point 3).
2 — Backend Routes: Vehicle Route File
No vehicle route file exists. The routes directory contains:
auth.ts  health.ts  index.ts  lookup.ts  pricing.ts
stakeholders.ts  states.ts  trips.ts  users.ts  wallets.ts
No vehicles.ts file is present.
3 — Trips Table Columns
id                      UUID PK
vehicle_number          VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL      ← plain text, no FK
driver_id               UUID → users(id)
loading_state_id        UUID → states(id)
unloading_state_id      UUID → states(id)
origin_description      TEXT (nullable)
destination_description TEXT (nullable)
status                  VARCHAR(50) DEFAULT 'created'
created_by              UUID → users(id)
deleted_at              TIMESTAMP (nullable)
created_at              TIMESTAMP
updated_at              TIMESTAMP
Vehicle reference: plain text vehicle_number VARCHAR(50). There is no vehicle_id foreign key — no vehicles table to point to.
4 — createTrip — Request Body & Tables Written
Fields accepted in the request body:
Field    Type    Required
vehicleNumber    string    ✅
driverId    string (UUID)    ✅
loadingStateId    string (UUID)    ✅
unloadingStateId    string (UUID)    ✅
originDescription    string    optional
destinationDescription    string    optional
fees.loadingFee    integer (kobo)    ✅
fees.unloadingFee    integer (kobo)    ✅
fees.stakeholders[]    array of {stakeholderId, amount, enabled}    ✅ (can be empty)
Tables written to (inside a single transaction):
trips — inserts one row
trip_fees — inserts one row per fee entry (loading + unloading + each enabled stakeholder)
wallets — updates total_collected for each fee recipient
wallet_ledger — inserts one 'credit' entry per fee
trip_events — inserts one 'created' event
It also performs existence checks against drivers, states, and stakeholders before opening the transaction, and checks trips for a duplicate active vehicle_number (status created or in_transit).

├── _layout.tsx   — Stack layout with header "Synergy Cargo Tax" + logout button (added in the last fix)
└── index.tsx     — Placeholder: renders a centred View with the text "Trip Admin Home". No navigation, no data, no actions.
Both files are stubs. The Trip Admin has no real UI yet.

Architected comprehensive vehicle management feature with migrations, APIs, and tests
Architected comprehensive vehicle management feature with migrations, APIs, and tests
Paste this into the Replit Agent:

I need to build the vehicle management feature. This covers a database migration, backend CRUD and search, a reusable frontend search component, and a Trip Admin vehicles screen. Follow all steps in order.

Step 1 -- Database migration

Open artifacts/api-server/src/db/migrate.ts. Make the following changes:

Add a vehicles table after the drivers table block:

sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS vehicles (
  id                  UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  registration_number VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
  created_by          UUID REFERENCES users(id),
  is_active           BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT true,
  deleted_at          TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE,
  created_at          TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
  updated_at          TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);
Then add a migration block that:

Inserts one row into vehicles for each distinct vehicle_number currently in the trips table, using ON CONFLICT (registration_number) DO NOTHING to keep it idempotent
Adds a vehicle_id UUID column to trips referencing vehicles(id) if it does not already exist
Updates each trip's vehicle_id by matching trips.vehicle_number to vehicles.registration_number
sql
-- Migrate existing vehicle numbers into vehicles table
INSERT INTO vehicles (registration_number)
SELECT DISTINCT vehicle_number FROM trips WHERE vehicle_number IS NOT NULL
ON CONFLICT (registration_number) DO NOTHING;

-- Add vehicle_id FK column to trips
ALTER TABLE trips ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS vehicle_id UUID REFERENCES vehicles(id);

-- Backfill vehicle_id on existing trips
UPDATE trips t SET vehicle_id = v.id
FROM vehicles v WHERE t.vehicle_number = v.registration_number;
Update the success log from All 11 tables to All 12 tables. Run the migration:

pnpm --filter @workspace/api-server run migrate
Confirm the vehicles table exists and all existing trips have a vehicle_id populated.

Step 2 -- Backend controller

Create artifacts/api-server/src/controllers/vehicleController.ts with these functions:

createVehicle -- POST handler. Accepts registration_number. Checks for an existing vehicle with the same registration number (including soft-deleted). If found active, returns 409. If found soft-deleted, reactivates it. Otherwise inserts a new row. Sets created_by to the calling user's id. Returns the created or reactivated vehicle.

searchVehicles -- GET handler for /vehicles/search?q=. Accepts a query string q. Returns all vehicles where registration_number ILIKE '%q%' and deleted_at IS NULL, ordered by closest match first. Returns up to 10 results. Used by the trip creation search dropdown.

listVehicles -- GET handler for /vehicles. Returns all active vehicles created by the calling Trip Admin (filter by created_by = req.user.id). Includes total trip count per vehicle by joining the trips table.

deleteVehicle -- DELETE handler. Soft deletes a vehicle by setting deleted_at = NOW(). Returns 400 if the vehicle has any active trips (status created or in_transit).

Step 3 -- Backend route

Create artifacts/api-server/src/routes/vehicles.ts:

GET    /vehicles           authenticateToken + requireRole("trip_admin") → listVehicles
POST   /vehicles           authenticateToken + requireRole("trip_admin") → createVehicle
GET    /vehicles/search    authenticateToken + requireRole("trip_admin") → searchVehicles
DELETE /vehicles/:id       authenticateToken + requireRole("trip_admin") → deleteVehicle
Register this router in artifacts/api-server/src/app.ts under the /api/vehicles prefix.

Step 4 -- Update createTrip to use vehicle_id

Open artifacts/api-server/src/controllers/tripController.ts. Update the createTrip function:

Instead of accepting vehicleNumber as a free-text string, accept vehicleId (UUID)
Look up the vehicle in the vehicles table by id to confirm it exists and is active
Write vehicle_id to the trips table instead of vehicle_number
Keep the duplicate active trip check but base it on vehicle_id instead of vehicle_number
Keep vehicle_number column on the trips table for now -- do not remove it yet
Step 5 -- Reusable VehicleSearchInput component

Create artifacts/mobile/components/VehicleSearchInput.tsx. This component:

Accepts props: value, onSelect(vehicle), onRegisterNew(registrationNumber)
Renders a TextInput where the Trip Admin types a registration number
After each keystroke (debounced 300ms) calls GET /api/vehicles/search?q= and shows results in a dropdown below the input
Each result row shows the registration number and is tappable to select
If no results are found, shows an "Register [typed value] as new vehicle" option at the bottom of the dropdown
Tapping that option calls onRegisterNew with the typed registration number, which the parent form will use to create the vehicle inline
Follows the existing design system: white background, primary color 
#1a6ec8, card-based dropdown
Step 6 -- Trip Admin screens

Replace the placeholder content in artifacts/mobile/app/(tripadmin)/index.tsx with a proper home screen that has two tabs: Trips (empty placeholder for now -- comes in Feature 4) and Vehicles.

The Vehicles tab should:

Fetch and display the Trip Admin's vehicles using GET /api/vehicles
Show each vehicle as a card with its registration number and trip count
Include a FAB to register a new vehicle, which opens a simple modal with a single registration number field
On submit call POST /api/vehicles, refresh the list on success
Show inline error if the registration number already exists
Use the VehicleSearchInput component inside the modal so the Trip Admin can search first before registering
Update artifacts/mobile/app/(tripadmin)/_layout.tsx to support the tab structure if needed.

Add these API helpers to artifacts/mobile/services/api.ts:

searchVehicles(q: string)
createVehicle(body)
listVehicles()
deleteVehicle(id)
Step 7 -- TypeScript check

Exact file and lines changed
File: artifacts/mobile/app/(tripadmin)/vehicles.tsx — no other file touched.
Lines    What changed
28    Added `const [fetchError, setFetchError] = useState<string
70    Added setFetchError(null); on successful fetch
72    Replaced silent // silently keep existing list on error with setFetchError("Couldn't load your vehicles");
130–144    New early-return block: renders centered cloud-offline icon + error message + Retry button when fetchError is set and vehicles.length === 0
293–311    Added fetchErrorTitle, retryButton, retryButtonText style entries
Error state vs empty state — mutually exclusive
They are structurally separate — not both rendered conditionally inside the same tree. The logic is:
if (loading)        → spinner (early return, nothing else renders)
if (fetchError && vehicles.length === 0) → error state (early return, nothing else renders)
// falls through to FlatList
  ListEmptyComponent → "No vehicles yet" (only reachable when fetch succeeded and returned [])
Only one of these three paths is ever active. The empty state text "No vehicles yet" is inside ListEmptyComponent, which is only reached via the FlatList path — which is only reached when both early returns were skipped, meaning the fetch succeeded.

I need to build Feature 4 -- Trip Admin trip creation. This includes a driver search and registration backend, a DriverSearchInput component, and the full trip creation form. Follow all steps in order.

Step 1 -- Backend: driver controller

Create artifacts/api-server/src/controllers/driverController.ts with two functions:

createDriver -- POST handler. Accepts full_name, license_number, phone_number. Checks for an existing driver with the same license_number (including soft-deleted). If found active, returns 409. If found soft-deleted, reactivates it and updates the name/phone. Otherwise inserts a new row. Sets created_by to the calling user's id. Returns the created driver.

searchDrivers -- GET handler for /drivers/search?q=. Accepts a query string q. Returns all drivers where full_name ILIKE '%q%' OR license_number ILIKE '%q%' OR phone_number ILIKE '%q%' and deleted_at IS NULL. Returns up to 10 results.

Create artifacts/api-server/src/routes/drivers.ts:

POST   /drivers           authenticateToken + requireRole("trip_admin") → createDriver
GET    /drivers/search    authenticateToken + requireRole("trip_admin") → searchDrivers
Register this router in artifacts/api-server/src/app.ts under the /api/drivers prefix.

Step 3 -- Frontend: DriverSearchInput component

Create artifacts/mobile/components/DriverSearchInput.tsx, mirroring the structure of VehicleSearchInput.tsx with these differences:

Search is by name, license number, or phone number combined (single text input, debounced 300ms, calls GET /api/drivers/search?q=)
Each result row shows the driver's full name with license number and phone as secondary text
When no results are found, instead of a single "Register as new" row, show a "Register new driver" row that expands an inline mini-form with three fields: Full Name, License Number, Phone Number
The inline form has its own Submit button that calls POST /api/drivers, and on success calls onSelect with the newly created driver and collapses the form

Export a Driver interface: { id, fullName, licenseNumber, phoneNumber, createdBy, isActive, createdAt }
Follow the same design system and styling patterns as VehicleSearchInput.tsx

On mount, fetch GET /api/pricing/active and store the loading fee, unloading fee, and stakeholder rates

Architected multi-tier trip cancellation workflow with approval gates
Architected multi-tier trip cancellation workflow with approval gates
Good findings. A few design decisions need your input before I write the build prompt, since this feature has more moving parts than the previous ones.

1. The existing cancelTrip endpoint is super_admin-only with no approval flow. This predates the pivot and does not match the SOW at all -- Super Admin should not be involved in cancellation per the current requirements. My plan is to leave this old endpoint as-is for now (do not touch it, do not expose it anywhere in the UI) and build the new Trip Admin → State Admin flow as separate endpoints. We can clean up or remove the old one in Stage 3. Does that sound right, or do you want it removed now?

2. New cancellation_pending status needs to be added, plus we need to track what the trip's status was before cancellation was requested (created or in_transit) so we can revert it correctly if the State Admin rejects the request.

3. Pre-approved cancellation rights will be a new boolean column on the users table, only meaningful for trip_admin role, settable only by the State Admin who owns that Trip Admin.

Proposed design:

New table cancellation_requests:

id, trip_id, requested_by (Trip Admin), reason, status (pending/approved/rejected), previous_trip_status, reviewed_by (State Admin), rejection_reason, timestamps
New column on users: can_cancel_without_approval BOOLEAN DEFAULT false

New endpoints:

POST /trips/:tripId/request-cancellation -- Trip Admin only, must own the trip. Body: reason. If the calling Trip Admin has can_cancel_without_approval = true, cancel immediately (reuse the existing wallet reversal logic). Otherwise create a cancellation_requests row with status pending and set trip status to cancellation_pending.
GET /cancellation-requests -- State Admin only, scoped to Trip Admins in their state, returns pending requests
PATCH /cancellation-requests/:id/approve -- State Admin only. Cancels the trip with wallet reversal, marks request approved.
PATCH /cancellation-requests/:id/reject -- State Admin only. Body: optional rejection_reason. Reverts trip status to previous_trip_status, marks request rejected.
PATCH /users/:id/cancellation-rights -- State Admin only, can only target their own Trip Admins. Body: can_cancel_without_approval: boolean.
Does this design look right before I write the full build prompt?

Paste this into the Replit Agent:

I need to build the full cancellation flow feature, including removing an old endpoint that no longer matches requirements. Follow all steps in order.

Step 1 -- Remove the old super_admin cancellation endpoint

Open artifacts/api-server/src/routes/trips.ts and artifacts/api-server/src/controllers/tripController.ts.

Remove the PATCH /:tripId/cancel route entirely from trips.ts. Remove the cancelTrip function entirely from tripController.ts. This endpoint was super_admin only and predates the current requirement that only Trip Admin (with approval) or pre-approved Trip Admin can cancel trips.

Before removing, note exactly what the wallet reversal logic inside cancelTrip does (fetching credit ledger entries, inserting reversal entries, decrementing wallet totals) -- we will reuse this same logic in the new cancellation approval endpoint in Step 3.

Also remove the unused cancelTrip helper stub from artifacts/mobile/services/api.ts if it exists and is not used anywhere.

Step 2 -- Database migration

Open artifacts/api-server/src/db/migrate.ts. Make these changes:

Add a cancellation_requests table after the trip_events table block:

sql
CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS cancellation_requests (
  id                    UUID PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  trip_id               UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES trips(id),
  requested_by          UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES users(id),
  reason                TEXT NOT NULL,
  status                VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
  previous_trip_status  VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
  reviewed_by           UUID REFERENCES users(id),
  rejection_reason      TEXT,
  created_at            TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW(),
  updated_at            TIMESTAMP WITH TIME ZONE NOT NULL DEFAULT NOW()
);
Add a new column to users:

sql
ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS can_cancel_without_approval BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false;
Update the success log from All 12 tables to All 13 tables. Run the migration and confirm both changes applied successfully.

Step 3 -- Backend: cancellation controller

Create artifacts/api-server/src/controllers/cancellationController.ts with these functions:

requestCancellation -- PATCH handler for /trips/:tripId/request-cancellation. Trip Admin only. Validates:

The trip exists, is not already completed or cancelled or cancellation_pending
The trip was created by the calling Trip Admin (check trips.created_by)
Body requires a reason (non-empty string)
Logic:

Look up the calling user's can_cancel_without_approval flag
If true: immediately cancel the trip. Reuse the exact wallet reversal logic from the old cancelTrip (fetch credit ledger entries for the trip, insert reversal entries, decrement wallet totals), set trip status = 'cancelled', set deleted_at = NOW(), insert a trip_events row with event_type: 'cancelled'. All inside a transaction. Return the cancelled trip.
If false: insert a row into cancellation_requests with status: 'pending', previous_trip_status set to the trip's current status, reason from the body. Update the trip's status to cancellation_pending. Insert a trip_events row with event_type: 'cancellation_requested' and metadata containing the reason. Return the created cancellation request.
listCancellationRequests -- GET handler for /cancellation-requests. State Admin only. Returns all pending cancellation requests where the trip's created_by user has entity_id matching the calling State Admin's entity_id. Join trip details (vehicle registration, driver name, route) and the requesting Trip Admin's email for display.

approveCancellation -- PATCH handler for /cancellation-requests/:id/approve. State Admin only. Validates the request exists, is pending, and belongs to a Trip Admin in the caller's state (via the same entity_id join as above). Inside a transaction: run the same wallet reversal logic, set the trip status = 'cancelled' and deleted_at = NOW(), update the cancellation request status = 'approved' and reviewed_by = caller.id, insert a trip_events row with event_type: 'cancellation_approved'. Return the updated request.

rejectCancellation -- PATCH handler for /cancellation-requests/:id/reject. State Admin only. Same ownership validation as approve. Accepts optional rejection_reason in body. Reverts the trip's status back to previous_trip_status from the request row. Updates the cancellation request status = 'rejected', rejection_reason, reviewed_by = caller.id. Insert a trip_events row with event_type: 'cancellation_rejected'. Return the updated request.

Step 4 -- Backend: cancellation rights controller addition

Add a function updateCancellationRights to userController.ts (or create if a more appropriate file exists) -- PATCH handler for /users/:id/cancellation-rights. State Admin only. Validates the target user has role = 'trip_admin' and entity_id matches the caller's entity_id (i.e. the State Admin can only modify their own Trip Admins). Accepts can_cancel_without_approval: boolean in body. Updates the column. Returns the updated user (without password hash).

Step 5 -- Backend routes

Add to artifacts/api-server/src/routes/trips.ts:

PATCH /:tripId/request-cancellation   authenticateToken + requireRole("trip_admin") → requestCancellation
Create artifacts/api-server/src/routes/cancellationRequests.ts:

GET    /cancellation-requests              authenticateToken + requireRole("state_admin") → listCancellationRequests
PATCH  /cancellation-requests/:id/approve  authenticateToken + requireRole("state_admin") → approveCancellation
PATCH  /cancellation-requests/:id/reject   authenticateToken + requireRole("state_admin") → rejectCancellation
Register in app.ts.

Add to artifacts/api-server/src/routes/users.ts:

PATCH /:id/cancellation-rights   authenticateToken + requireRole("state_admin") → updateCancellationRights
Step 6 -- Frontend: Trip Admin cancellation request UI

In artifacts/mobile/app/(tripadmin)/index.tsx, on each trip card in the trips list (only for trips with status created or in_transit), add a "Request Cancellation" action. Tapping it opens a small modal with a required reason text field and a Submit button. On submit, call the new request-cancellation endpoint. Show appropriate feedback:

If the Trip Admin has pre-approved rights, the trip should immediately show as cancelled after submission
If not, the trip should show as cancellation_pending with a distinct badge color
Add cancellation_pending and a clear color to the status badge styling, consistent with the existing cancelled, created, in_transit, completed badges
Add a requestCancellation(tripId, reason) helper to services/api.ts.

Step 7 -- Frontend: State Admin cancellation approval screen

Replace the placeholder in artifacts/mobile/app/(stateadmin)/index.tsx with a real screen showing a list of pending cancellation requests (GET /api/cancellation-requests). Each card shows: trip details (vehicle, driver, route), the reason given, and the requesting Trip Admin's email. Include Approve and Reject buttons on each card. Reject should open a small modal for an optional rejection reason before confirming. On action, call the appropriate endpoint and refresh the list.

Flagged migration script deviation and verified naming conventions
The plan is comprehensive and matches the design correctly. One important thing to flag before you approve.

Migration concern: The plan says it will apply the cancellation_requests table and the new users column "directly via executeSql" because of a tsconfig issue preventing migrate.ts from running normally. This is fine for getting the database into the right state right now, but it must not replace updating the actual migrate.ts source file. If the SQL is only run ad-hoc and not also added to the migration script itself, then any future database reset (a new environment, a fresh Replit instance, etc.) will be missing these tables entirely -- same class of bug we caught back in Stage 1 with the drivers table.

Before approving, add this clarification to the agent's task. Paste this addition along with your approval:

Before proceeding: confirm that the cancellation_requests table SQL and the can_cancel_without_approval column SQL are being added directly into the migrate.ts source file as permanent blocks (same pattern as all previous tables), not just executed once via a direct SQL call. The direct execution is only acceptable as the way to apply it to the current live database given the tsconfig issue -- the source file itself must still contain these blocks so that any future fresh migration run creates them. Confirm this explicitly in your response before starting the rest of the task.

Everything else in the plan is correct -- the wallet reversal logic duplication is acceptable here since the original cancelTrip is being deleted entirely, the state-scoping via entity_id is consistent with how Feature 2 already enforces State Admin boundaries, and the cancellation_pending badge getting a distinct amber/orange color avoids confusion with existing statuses.

Here are the findings across all five questions:
1 — State Admin screen structure
The (stateadmin)/ group currently has exactly two files: _layout.tsx and index.tsx. There are no other screens or tabs.
The layout uses <Stack> (not <Tabs>), so the State Admin has no tab structure at all — it is a single screen. The logout button is rendered directly in the Stack header's headerRight. This means adding a second section requires converting the layout from <Stack> to <Tabs tabBar={(props) => <PillTabBar {...props} />}>, exactly mirroring what Trip Admin does.
2 — Listing Trip Admins under a State Admin
GET /api/users exists but is restricted to super_admin only (line 9 of users.ts):
router.get("/", authenticateToken, requireRole("super_admin"), listUsers);
The listUsers controller returns all users regardless of any filter — it does a single SELECT with no WHERE clause on role or entity_id. A State Admin calling it would get a 403.
There is no endpoint a State Admin can currently call to list only the Trip Admins in their state. A new endpoint (or a role-aware variant of GET /api/users) will need to be added on the backend before the mobile screen can fetch this list.
3 — Cancellation rights endpoint
Request body (PATCH /api/users/:id/cancellation-rights):
{ "canCancelWithoutApproval": true }   // boolean, required
Response body (200):
{
  "data": { "id": "...", "email": "...", "canCancelWithoutApproval": true },
  "message": "Cancellation rights granted"   // or "revoked"
}
Guard logic: validates target is role = trip_admin, confirms target.entity_id === caller.entityId (cross-state blocked → 403), updates users.can_cancel_without_approval.
Mobile usage: updateCancellationRights(userId, bool) is defined in services/api.ts (lines 109–110) but is not imported or called from any screen anywhere in the mobile app — it exists only as a helper function.
4 — Trip Admin onboarding flow location
POST /api/users is fully implemented on the backend (State Admin can create a trip_admin linked to their own state). However, there is no frontend form anywhere in (stateadmin)/. The index.tsx is entirely the cancellation requests approval screen with no "create user" modal, button, or navigation. This capability is backend-only so far.
5 — PillTabBar pattern (from (tripadmin)/_layout.tsx)
The Trip Admin layout is the exact pattern to mirror:
import PillTabBar from '@/components/PillTabBar';
export default function TripAdminLayout() {
  return (
    <Tabs
      tabBar={(props) => <PillTabBar {...props} />}
      screenOptions={{ /* same headerTitle/headerRight/headerStyle block */ }}
    >
      <Tabs.Screen name="index"    options={{ title: 'Trips',    tabBarLabel: 'Trips' }} />
      <Tabs.Screen name="vehicles" options={{ title: 'Vehicles', tabBarLabel: 'Vehicles' }} />
    </Tabs>
  );
}
PillTabBar is a shared component at @/components/PillTabBar. The logout button stays in screenOptions.headerRight (same pattern). For State Admin the two tabs would be index (Cancellation Requests) and a new trip-admins screen.
Summary of what needs to be built:
Layer    Work needed
Backend    New endpoint — e.g. GET /api/users/trip-admins — accessible to state_admin, filtered by their entity_id and can_cancel_without_approval flag included in response
(stateadmin)/_layout.tsx    Convert <Stack> → <Tabs tabBar={PillTabBar}> with two screens
(stateadmin)/index.tsx    Remove the inline logout button (it moves to layout's headerRight)
(stateadmin)/trip-admins.tsx    New screen: list of Trip Admins with pre-approval toggle + create TA form

Acknowledged validation completion and refined future testing protocols
Acknowledged validation completion and refined future testing protocols
All 10 tests pass. Feature 6 is complete.

Good catch on the URL navigation issue -- that is an important flaw to flag. If the agent types a URL directly to "get to" a screen instead of tapping the actual tab or button, it can completely miss real bugs like the PillTabBar touch-interception issue we found back in Stage 1. The whole point of App Testing is to catch exactly that kind of thing. Going forward, I will explicitly instruct the agent to navigate only by tapping visible UI elements (buttons, tabs, links) and never by directly entering or modifying the URL, and to flag it as a test failure if it cannot find a tappable element to reach a screen rather than working around it.

On the two follow-up items the agent flagged (#88 disable/reactivate Trip Admin accounts, #89 trip activity count on cards) -- these are reasonable nice-to-haves but were not in the original SOW scope for this feature. I will note them in the .md file as backlog items for Stage 3 polish rather than building them now, to stay disciplined about scope.

Architected feature design while flagging critical product decisions
Architected feature design while flagging critical product decisions
Good findings, but two important design decisions came out of this that I want your input on before writing the build prompt.

Decision 1 -- Trip ID does not exist yet. The SOW requires a unique, human-readable Trip ID assigned at creation as the primary reference. Right now trips only have a raw UUID. We need to add a new column, something like trip_code, generated automatically at trip creation (e.g. format TRP-2026-000147 or similar). This means a small addition to the existing createTrip function in Feature 4's code. Are you fine with this format, or do you have a specific format in mind for the Trip ID?

Decision 2 -- Payment status has no real "paid" concept yet. This is the more important one. The wallet_ledger.cleared flag exists in the schema but is never set anywhere in the codebase -- nothing currently marks a fee as actually settled. The existing public vehicle lookup endpoint fakes this by treating "a fee was recorded" as "paid," which is not really accurate -- it just means the fee was assessed at trip creation, not that anyone confirmed it was paid out.

Given that EOD clearing logic is still an open, undefined item in our backlog, building out a real payment-clearing mechanism is out of scope for this feature. I see two honest paths forward:

Option A: Keep the current proxy behavior -- "fee recorded" displays as "Paid" on the public lookup, consistent with what already exists. Flag clearly in the .md file that this is a placeholder until real clearing logic is built.
Option B: Be more conservative -- show fees as "Pending" until cleared = true, which means every single fee will show as pending right now since nothing sets that flag yet, making the payment verification feature look broken or empty.
My recommendation is Option A, since it matches existing behavior and gives the client something meaningful to see in a demo, with the caveat clearly documented as a known gap to revisit. But this is worth being upfront with the client about, since "payment verification" was described as the primary purpose of this screen, and the underlying mechanism for confirming a payment was actually settled does not exist yet.

What would you like to do?

For the statement of the payment for, uh, the decision decision number two, you are asking. I think option is correct. Uh, option a is correct because, uh, right now, we are trying to create an MVP, and, uh, no actual payment will be made. There's no movement of money that could be happening with this application currently at this stage. Uh, this is... this this application will be, like, a very well working MVP, uh, which after approvals and, uh, compliances, we will be able to connect the actual payment processor who will... that will actually make payment on behalf of one party to another one in this application. So right now, I think option a would be a good choice. But make sure to leave some space or make it... make the system as such so that we can connect the payment system in future, and there's no extreme code refactoring that would be needed to accommodate that. Just... should we should we just, like, a modular which can come and connect? Make sure to leave some room for that as well. But right now, we just need to mock a lot of things. So option is correct. For decision number one, I think I think come up with something which is reasonable and followed industry wide. I don't have any specific format for the trip ID, so I think we should be able to think of something which is which cancel the requirement and also Follow certain format, which is accepted industry wide or is used in industry.

Jun 21

Architected modular trip ID format and payment abstraction system
Architected modular trip ID format and payment abstraction system
Good guidance on both. For the Trip ID, I will go with a format that is clean, sortable, and consistent with how invoice or reference numbers are typically structured in industry: a prefix, the year, and a zero-padded sequential number. For example: SYN-2026-000147. This is readable, easy to communicate verbally or via SMS, sorts naturally by year, and avoids exposing the raw UUID anywhere public-facing.

For the payment status modularity, I will structure it as a single isolated function the rest of the codebase calls into, rather than scattering "if fee row exists, it's paid" logic across multiple files. That way, when the real payment processor gets connected later, only that one function needs to change.

Paste this into the Replit Agent:

I need to build the public trip lookup feature, including a new human-readable Trip ID, a payment status abstraction layer designed for future extensibility, and the full search experience. Follow all steps in order.

Step 1 -- Database migration: Trip ID

Open artifacts/api-server/src/db/migrate.ts. Add a trip_code column to the trips table:

sql
ALTER TABLE trips ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS trip_code VARCHAR(30) UNIQUE;
Add a sequence to generate the numeric portion atomically:

sql
CREATE SEQUENCE IF NOT EXISTS trip_code_seq START 1;
Add a migration block that backfills trip_code for all existing trips that do not have one, ordered by created_at ascending, using the format SYN-{year_of_created_at}-{6-digit zero-padded sequence}. Use nextval('trip_code_seq') for each row to assign the number atomically.

Update the success log appropriately. Run the migration and confirm every existing trip now has a unique trip_code.

Step 2 -- Update createTrip to generate trip_code

In tripController.ts, update createTrip so that every new trip generates its trip_code at insert time using the same format: fetch nextval('trip_code_seq'), get the current year, format as SYN-{year}-{padded(seq, 6)}. Include tripCode in the response of the created trip.

Step 3 -- Backend: payment status abstraction

Create a new file artifacts/api-server/src/services/paymentStatusService.ts. This file should export a single function, something like:

ts
export function getFeePaymentStatus(tripFee: { id: string; cleared: boolean }): { status: 'paid' | 'pending'; paidAt: string | null }
For now, the implementation should treat a fee as 'paid' simply because a trip_fees row exists for it (matching current behavior), with paidAt set to the trip's created_at as a placeholder. Add a clear comment block at the top of this file explaining: this is currently a placeholder that treats "fee recorded" as "fee paid" because no real payment processor is connected yet; this is the single integration point where real payment/clearing status (e.g. from wallet_ledger.cleared, or a future external payment processor webhook) should be wired in later, so that no other file needs to change when that happens.

Use this function in all payment status lookups going forward instead of inline existence checks.

Step 4 -- Backend: public trip search endpoint

Create artifacts/api-server/src/controllers/publicLookupController.ts (or add to the existing lookup controller) with a searchTrips function. GET handler for /lookup/trips/search?q=. No auth required, but apply the existing publicLookupLimiter rate limiting middleware.

Logic:

Accept a single query parameter q
Search across: trip_code (exact or prefix match), drivers.full_name, drivers.license_number, drivers.phone_number, trips.vehicle_number, trips.origin_description, trips.destination_description, loading and unloading state names, and trips.created_at (if q parses as a valid date, match trips created on that date)
Use a single query with LEFT JOIN drivers, LEFT JOIN states (twice, aliased for loading/unloading), matching any field with ILIKE '%q%' combined with OR
Return up to 20 results, most recent first
For each result, also fetch its trip_fees rows and apply getFeePaymentStatus from Step 3 to each one, returning a paymentStatus object: { loading: 'paid'|'pending', unloading: 'paid'|'pending', stakeholders: [{ stakeholderId, stakeholderName, status }] }
Each result should include: tripCode, status (trip status), vehicleNumber, driverName (or null), loadingStateName, unloadingStateName, originDescription, destinationDescription, createdAt, paymentStatus
Register this route in artifacts/api-server/src/routes/lookup.ts as GET /trips/search, no authenticateToken, with publicLookupLimiter applied. Do not remove or modify the existing GET /lookup/vehicle/:vehicleNumber endpoint -- leave it exactly as-is for backward compatibility, but note in a comment that it is now superseded by the new search endpoint.

Step 5 -- Frontend: public lookup screen

A search bar at the top with placeholder text like "Search by Trip ID, driver, vehicle, license, phone, or location"
Debounced search (400ms) calling GET /api/lookup/trips/search?q=
Results displayed as cards, each showing: Trip ID, vehicle number, driver name, route (loading state → unloading state), trip status badge, and date
Tapping a result card expands it (or navigates to a detail view) showing the full payment verification breakdown: Loading Fee -- Paid/Pending badge, Unloading Fee -- Paid/Pending badge, and one row per stakeholder with their name and Paid/Pending badge
Use green for "Paid" and amber/orange for "Pending" badges, consistent with existing badge color patterns in the app
Show an empty state when no search has been entered yet ("Search for a trip to verify payment status")
Show a "No results found" state when a search returns nothing
Show a loading indicator while searching
This screen requires no login -- confirm it renders correctly for both authenticated and unauthenticated users, consistent with how (public) already works
Follow the existing design system throughout

Jun 21

Analyzed sequence capacity, overflow risks, and uniqueness guarantees thoroughly
Analyzed sequence capacity, overflow risks, and uniqueness guarantees thoroughly
Good question to catch before we build this — that is exactly the kind of edge case worth nailing down upfront.

Here is how it works and why it is actually fully safe:

The sequence itself has no real limit. I designed it using a PostgreSQL SEQUENCE, which is a 64-bit number under the hood — it can count up to over 9 quintillion before it would ever run out. That is effectively unlimited for any realistic scale this platform will ever reach.

The "6-digit" part is just a display minimum, not a cap. The padding only controls how many leading zeros are shown for small numbers (so trip 1 shows as 000001 instead of just 1). If the count ever crosses 999,999 trips, the code does not break, truncate, or wrap around — it simply shows more digits naturally, like SYN-2026-1000000. It stays unique and keeps counting normally.

One design decision I want to confirm with you: the sequence number does not reset each year in my current design — it just keeps counting forward forever, and the year is just a label showing when the trip was created. This means you will never get two trips with the same number even across different years, with zero risk of overlap, and it is the simplest and safest option.

The alternative — resetting the counter to 000001 at the start of every year, which is common in invoice numbering — looks cleaner in year one but adds real complexity: you would need a separate sequence per year and extra logic to guarantee uniqueness when combined with the year label, which is more moving parts for a problem we do not actually have at this stage.

My recommendation is to keep the simple non-resetting version. Does that work for you, or would you prefer the per-year reset version despite the added complexity?

yes keep the simple non-resetting version

Validated existing approach and confirmed readiness
Validated existing approach and confirmed readiness
Good, that confirms the prompt I gave you already matches this — the trip_code_seq sequence I specified is a single global sequence, not reset per year, so it is already built exactly the way you want. No changes needed.

Now here is the comprehensive testing prompt. This is large by necessity since it needs to cover the entire app. Paste this into the Replit Agent:

This is a full regression and discovery pass across the entire application -- both backend and frontend. The goal is to find every issue, gap, or broken flow across everything built so far. Do not fix anything you find. Only document it. We will triage and fix everything afterward in a separate pass.

For all frontend testing: perform live App Testing in an actual browser session, navigating only by tapping real, visible UI elements (buttons, tabs, links, list items). Never navigate by directly typing or editing a URL to jump between in-app screens -- the only exception is the very first page load of the app itself. If you cannot find a tappable way to reach a screen, report that itself as a finding.

Work through every section below in order. For each numbered test, report: what you did, what you expected, what actually happened, and PASS / FAIL / PARTIAL. If something fails, capture the exact error message, status code, or screen state.

SECTION A -- Authentication and Core Session

A1. Login with valid credentials for each of the four roles (super_admin, state_admin, trip_admin, stakeholder). Confirm correct landing screen for each.
A2. Login with an invalid password. Confirm a clear error message, no crash.
A3. Confirm JWT refresh works -- stay on an authenticated screen long enough to require a token refresh (or inspect the refresh logic) and confirm no forced logout occurs.
A4. Logout from each role. Confirm it returns to the login screen and a subsequent back-navigation does not re-enter an authenticated screen.
A5. Attempt to access a role-specific API endpoint with no token at all. Confirm 401.
A6. Attempt to access another role's endpoint using a valid token for the wrong role (e.g. Trip Admin token calling a Super Admin-only endpoint). Confirm 403.

SECTION B -- Super Admin Flows

B1. Login as Super Admin. Confirm the three tabs (Trips, Config, Users) all load without error.
B2. Trips tab: confirm the trip list displays and filter pills work.
B3. Config tab (pricing chart): view the current active chart. Create a new chart with different rates. Activate it. Confirm the previously active chart is now inactive and the new one is active.
B4. Users tab: view the user list, confirm role badges are correct for every user type present.
B5. Create a new State Admin via the modal. Confirm it appears in the list immediately.
B6. Create a new Stakeholder via the modal. Confirm it appears in the list immediately.
B7. Attempt to create a Trip Admin from this screen (should not be possible -- confirm the role picker does not even offer this option).
B8. Backend: confirm GET /api/users, GET /api/states, GET /api/stakeholders, GET /api/wallets/all all work correctly for Super Admin and return sensible data.

SECTION C -- State Admin Flows

C1. Login as a State Admin. Confirm the two tabs (Requests, Trip Admins) load.
C2. Trip Admins tab: confirm only Trip Admins belonging to this State Admin's own state appear.
C3. Toggle pre-approved cancellation rights on a Trip Admin. Confirm it persists after a refresh.
C4. Create a new Trip Admin via the modal. Confirm it appears immediately and is correctly scoped to this State Admin's state.
C5. Requests tab: if there are no pending cancellation requests, confirm the empty state displays correctly.
C6. Backend: confirm a State Admin cannot list or modify Trip Admins belonging to a different state (re-verify cross-state isolation still holds after all the features built since Feature 6).

SECTION D -- Trip Admin Flows (the most complex role)

D1. Login as a Trip Admin. Confirm the two tabs (Trips, Vehicles) load.
D2. Vehicles tab: confirm the vehicle list loads, shows correct trip counts.
D3. Register a new vehicle via the search-and-register flow. Confirm it appears in the list.
D4. Search for an existing vehicle by partial registration number. Confirm matching results appear in the dropdown.
D5. Trips tab: confirm the trip list loads with correct status badges.
D6. Create a full new trip: select an existing vehicle via search, register a brand new driver inline via the driver search component, select loading and unloading states, confirm pricing chart values are correctly pre-filled and read-only, toggle a stakeholder off, submit. Confirm the trip appears in the list with status created and a properly formatted trip_code.
D7. Attempt to submit the trip creation form with a required field missing (e.g. no vehicle selected). Confirm a clear validation error and no submission occurs.
D8. On the newly created trip, tap Request Cancellation. Enter a reason and submit. Confirm the trip status updates correctly based on whether this Trip Admin has pre-approved rights or not.
D9. If this Trip Admin does NOT have pre-approved rights: confirm the trip now shows cancellation_pending and cannot be cancelled again or have its status changed further until a State Admin acts on it.
D10. Backend: confirm a Trip Admin cannot see or act on vehicles, drivers, or trips created by a different Trip Admin (re-verify cross-admin isolation holds).
D11. Backend: attempt to create a trip with a vehicleId that does not exist. Confirm a clear validation error, not a server crash.
D12. Backend: attempt to create a trip reusing a vehicle that already has another active (created or in_transit) trip. Confirm the duplicate-active-trip guard still works correctly.

SECTION E -- Cancellation Approval Flow (cross-role)

E1. As the State Admin for the Trip Admin from Section D, login and check the Requests tab. Confirm the cancellation request from D8/D9 appears (if it was a request, not an instant cancellation).
E2. Approve it. Confirm the trip status becomes cancelled, and confirm via direct database or wallet endpoint check that the wallet credits for that trip were correctly reversed.
E3. Create another trip and cancellation request as Trip Admin. As State Admin, reject it with a reason. Confirm the trip status reverts correctly to what it was before the cancellation request (not stuck on cancellation_pending, not incorrectly marked cancelled).

SECTION F -- Stakeholder Flows

F1. Login as a Stakeholder. Confirm the single home screen loads with wallet balance and ledger list, not the old placeholder.
F2. Confirm the wallet balance shown matches what was credited from trips created in earlier sections, accounting for any reversals from Section E.
F3. Use the date range filter. Confirm the list updates correctly.
F4. Pull to refresh. Confirm no crash and data reloads correctly.

SECTION G -- Public Lookup Flow

G1. From the login screen (not logged in), tap "Verify a trip payment". Confirm the screen loads with no auth prompt.
G2. Search for the trip created in Section D using its trip_code. Confirm it appears with the correct payment status.
G3. Search using the driver's name from that trip. Confirm the same trip appears.
G4. Search using the vehicle registration number. Confirm the same trip appears.
G5. Tap the result to expand payment status. Confirm Loading, Unloading, and stakeholder rows display with correct Paid/Pending badges.
G6. Search for the cancelled trip from Section E. Confirm its status and payment information display sensibly even though it was cancelled (decide if this is handled gracefully or not, and report exactly what is shown).
G7. Search for something with no matches. Confirm the "No results" state.

SECTION H -- Cross-Cutting Data Integrity Checks

H1. Query the database directly: confirm no trips exist with a null vehicle_id, null trip_code, or an invalid status value outside the five known values (created, in_transit, cancellation_pending, cancelled, completed).
H2. Confirm no users rows exist with role outside the four valid values.
H3. Confirm every wallet_ledger entry has a valid wallet_id, trip_id, and trip_fee_id reference (no orphaned rows).
H4. Confirm wallets.total_collected for every wallet matches the sum of its wallet_ledger entries (credits minus reversals) -- flag any wallet where these do not reconcile.

SECTION I -- Console and Stability

I1. Throughout all of the above, note any console errors, warnings, or layout children warnings encountered, even if the corresponding feature still appeared to function. List them all, grouped by which section/screen triggered them.

At the end, compile a single consolidated list of every FAIL and PARTIAL result across all sections, plus every console warning from Section I, formatted as a numbered backlog list ready for triage. Do not attempt to fix any of them.

Triaged bug findings, distinguished confirmed issues from ambiguous ones requiring investigation
This report surfaced some genuinely important findings, but a few of them need a closer look before we accept them as confirmed bugs and lock them into the backlog. Let me walk through the triage.

Confirmed real issues, ready for Stage 3A:

Vehicles tab "+" button is unclickable -- this is serious since it means Trip Admins currently cannot register new vehicles through the UI at all. What's concerning is that this button was specifically built to dodge the FAB pointer-events issue from Feature 3. If it's broken too, the underlying root cause may not be what we thought it was back in Stage 1.
Wallet total_collected reconciliation mismatch -- this is the most serious finding in the whole report, since it touches real money figures shown to State Admins and Stakeholders.
Needs investigation before we trust it, not a confirmed bug yet:

The wallet discrepancy numbers are large and suspicious (up to -₦46,000 on one wallet). Before treating this as a definite app bug, we need to rule out that the agent's own SQL query for this check has a join that's fan-out duplicating rows, which would inflate the "computed" side artificially. This needs a second pass that traces one specific wallet's full transaction history by hand.
The two driver role users -- these were intentionally soft-deleted in Feature 2 (deleted_at set, is_active = false), not actually removed, per our soft-delete architecture principle. The H2 check likely did not filter out soft-deleted rows. This is probably a false alarm, not a real bug, but worth confirming explicitly.
The logout-then-back-button landing on /__mockup -- this needs clarification on whether /__mockup is a real route in our app at all, or an artifact of the Expo web preview/dev tooling that would not exist in the actual Expo Go app or production build. If it's dev-tooling-only, it doesn't belong in our backlog as an app bug.
Testing gap that needs to be properly closed, not just accepted as "not tested":
A significant portion of the most complex role (Trip Admin trip creation, cancellation request) and the cancellation approval UI were never actually exercised in a live browser this round due to a routing issue landing sessions on /__mockup. The backend logic for these was already verified in earlier feature testing, but we should not leave Stage 2.5 calling itself "complete" while this much of the live UI is unverified.

Diagnosed testing bugs, isolated root cause, strategized remediation approach
Diagnosed testing bugs, isolated root cause, strategized remediation approach
Outstanding investigation. Let me walk through what this changes.

Three of the five findings are now closed as non-issues -- the wallet reconciliation alarm was a false positive caused by a double-negation bug in the test query itself (not the app), the driver role users are correctly soft-deleted as intended, and /__mockup is purely a Replit dev-environment artifact that won't exist in production. Good to have these definitively ruled out rather than carried forward as phantom backlog items.

One finding turned out to be more important than it first looked. The Vehicles "+" button issue isn't really about that one button -- the agent traced it to the PillTabBar wrapper itself, which is missing pointerEvents: 'box-none' and is silently defaulting to covering the entire screen height instead of just the tab bar area. This means the Stage 1 fix only addressed a secondary symptom (the slider), not the actual root cause. This matters because it likely affects any header button on any screen that uses PillTabBar -- not just Vehicles. Trip Admin's trip creation also uses a header "+" button per the same pattern (Feature 4), so it's likely also silently broken, which would explain why D6 onward couldn't be properly tested either way.

Given that, I think it's worth fixing this one specific, well-isolated root cause now -- as a deliberate exception to "discovery only" -- rather than running the remaining live tests against code we already know is broken and getting more failures that all trace back to the same fix. Once this is fixed, we re-run the full remaining live test sweep (D4, D6-D9, E1-E3, F3-F4, G6) in a single fresh session, avoiding A4 until the very end as the agent recommended.

Does that approach work for you, or would you rather we document the failures everywhere first and fix once at the end of Stage 2.5?
